\section{\ftwoz \ IOP for Lin-BitsRing}


\subsection{The core Lin-BitsRing relation}\label{s:linmodfieldbin}




\renewcommand{\ringtwo}{\FF_q}
\renewcommand{\ringmorphism}{\pi_q}
In this section we define the main linear relation we work with. We start by fixing notation:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
    \item Let $\comfield$ be a field with $2^{\comfieldexp}$ elements. Let
%
$
\beta_1, \ldots, \beta_\comfieldexp \in \comfield
$
%
be an $\FF_2$-basis of $\comfield$ seen as an $\FF_2$-vector space of dimension $\comfieldexp$. Given a vector $\uu\in \FF_2^\codedim$, with $\comfieldexp$ dividing $\codedim$, we define
%
$$
\pack(\uu)\in \comfield^{\codedim/\comfieldexp}
$$
as the vector whose $j$-th entry is
%
$$
\pack(\uu)_{j} = \uu_{(j-1)\cdot \comfieldexp +1 }\cdot \beta_1 + \ldots + \uu_{(j-1)\cdot \comfieldexp +\comfieldexp-1 }\cdot \beta_{\comfieldexp-1}+\uu_{j \cdot \comfieldexp} \cdot \beta_\comfieldexp \in \comfield.
$$
%
\item 
 Let $\codedim\geq 1$ be a dimension parameter divisible by $\comfieldexp$, $\cC$ a code over $\comfield$ of dimension $\codedim/\comfieldexp$ and blocklength $\codelength$.  
 \item  Let $R$ be a finitely generated ring (typically $R$ is $\ZZ$, a finite field, not necessarily a prime field, or a quotient of a polynomial ring $\ZZ[X]$ or $\FF_q[X]$) and let $\pi:\ZZ \to R$  be the  homomorphism mapping an integer $a$ to $a\cdot 1\in R$ (i.e.\ reduction modulo the characteristic of $R$).
\end{itemize}
 

\begin{definition} The \emph{Core Lin-BitsRing} relation  is defined as
%
$$
\LinBitsDual(\bits,\pi, R,\cC) \defeq \left\{ \left(\begin{aligned} &\inp=(\mu, \vv)\in R \times R^{\codedim}, \\&\oracle = f \in \comfield^\codelength, \\ &\wit= \bff\in \{0,1\}^\codedim \subseteq \ZZ^\codedim\end{aligned}\right) \;\middle|\;  \begin{aligned} &f=\Enc_{\cC}\circ \pack \circ \picomfield(\bff) \\    & \langle \pi(\bff), \vv\rangle  = \mu  \end{aligned} \right\}
$$
%
\end{definition}
%
%Again, when there is no risk of confusion, we write $\LinBitsDual(\bits,\ringmorphism, \moduletwo,\cC)$ simply as $\LinBitsDual$.
%
%
%
For $\delta\in (0,1)$, we let $\LinBitsDual^\delta$  be the relation $\LinBitsDual$  after replacing the constraint $f=\Enc_{\cC}\circ \pack\circ\pi_2(\bff)$ by
%
$$
\hamming(f,\Enc_{\cC}\circ \pack\circ\pi_2(\bff))<\delta.
$$
%
%
%
%
%

\renewcommand{\moduleone}{M}
\renewcommand{\moduleonebound}{M_{\leq B}}
\renewcommand{\moduletwo}{R}
 %For example, if $\ringtwo=\ZZ$, $\modulemorphism$ can be $\mathsf{id}$, and if $\ringtwo=\FF$, then $\modulemorphism$ can be $\pi_q$, i.e.\ reduction modulo $q$.


%When $Q$ and $\yy$ are such that $\yy=(\rr,\mu)\in \FF^{\log\codedim}\times \FF$ and $$Q(\yy,\ff)= \mle{\bff}(\rr)-\mu,$$ we denote $\Relation_{\cC, \FF}^{Q}$  by $\Relation_{\cC, \FF}^{\mathsf{mle}}$. 


%Next let $\comfield$ be a finite field of characteristic $2$,  and let $\moduletwo$ be either the field of rationals $\QQ$ or a finite field of characteristic $q$, possibly an extension field. For intuition, $\comfield$ is the field over which we will commit our witness, and $\moduletwo$ is the field over which we prove linear relations on the witness. If $\moduletwo=\QQ$ then we define $\degevalfield=0$, and if $\moduletwo$ is a finite field of characteristic $q$, then $\degevalfield$ denotes the degree extension of $\moduletwo$ over $\FF_q$.

%Let 
%
%$$
%\pievalfield: \ZZ[X] \to \moduletwo
%$$
%
%Rbe the natural projection of $\ZZ[X]$ onto $\moduletwo$. Concretely, if $\moduletwo=\QQ$,  then  $\pievalfield$ is reduction modulo the ideal $(X)$ (i.e.\ $\pievalfield(\sum_{i=0}^{k} a_i\cdot X^i)  = a_0$);  if $\moduletwo$ is a prime field $\FF_q$, then $\pievalfield$ is reduction modulo $q$ and modulo the ideal $(X)$; and if $\moduletwo$ is an extension field, so that $\moduletwo=\FF_q[X]/(h(X))$ for some irreducible polynomial $h(X)\in \FF_q[X]$, then $\pievalfield$ is reduction modulo $q$ and modulo the ideal $(h(X))$.




\renewcommand{\modulemorphism}{\psi}
\renewcommand{\moduletwo}{R}











%
\subsection{Tensor decomposition and products of constrained codes}\label{s:interleaved_setting}
%
\renewcommand{\ringmorphism}{\pi}
%\renewcommand{\ringtwo}{}




We start with some groundwork for our IOP for the Core Lin-BitsRing relation.  Let $(\inp,\oracle,\wit)=((\mu,\vv), f, \bff) \in \LinBitsDual(\bits,\ringmorphism, R,\cC)$. We fix a tensor decomposition of the vector $\vv\in \moduletwo^\codedim$ as $$\vv = \vv^{(1)} \otimes \vv^{(2)} = \left(\vv_i^{(1)}\cdot \vv_j^{(2)}\right)_{i\in [\codedim_1], j\in [\codedim_2]}$$ for some $\vv^{(1)}\in \moduletwo^{\codedim_1}, \vv^{(2)}\in \moduletwo^{\codedim_2}$, with $\codedim_1 \cdot \codedim_2=\codedim$. Note that the trivial tensor decomposition is admitted, i.e.\ we may effectively not decompose $\vv$ by choosing $\codedim_1=\codedim$, $\codedim_2=1$, $\vv^{(1)}=\vv$, and $\vv^{(2)} = (1)$.

%We assume $\cC = \cC_1^{\equiv \codedim_2}$ is the $\codedim_2$-dimensional interleaving of a code $\cC_1\subseteq \comfield^{\codelength/\codedim_2}$ of dimension $\codedim_1/2^\comfieldexp$ and blocklength $\codelength/\codedim_2$.

\renewcommand{\modulemorphism}{\ringmorphism}

We  index the entries of $\bff$ as $\bff=(\bff_{ij})_{i\in [\codedim_1], j\in[\codedim_2]}$. Note that
%
$$
\langle \modulemorphism(\bff), \vv \rangle = \sum_{j\in [\codedim_2]}\left(\sum_{i\in [\codedim_1]} \modulemorphism(\bff_{ij}) \cdot \vv_i^{(1)}\right) \cdot \vv_j^{(2)} = \sum_{j\in [\codedim_2]} \langle \modulemorphism(\bff_{*j}) , \vv^{(1)} \rangle \cdot \vv_j^{(2)},
$$
where $\bff_{*j} = (\bff_{ij})_{i\in[\codedim_1]}$. 
%
% We use $0$ and $1$ indistinctly to denote the zero and one integer, and zero and one field elements. 
%
Consider the relation  (cf.\ \cref{d:pesat}) $\PESAT(\comfield, Q_{\mathbb{B}\text{it}\ZZ},\cC, \pack)$ for $\ell_2$ constraint polynomials $Q_{\mathbb{B}\text{it}\ZZ}$ so that
%
\begin{align*}
&\PESAT(\comfield, Q_{\mathbb{B}\text{it}\ZZ},\cC, \pack)\\ &\qquad = \left\{ \left(\begin{aligned} &\inp=(\yy,\bm{\mu})\in \comfield^{\codedim_1}\times \comfield^{\codedim_2}, \\&\oracle = f\in \comfield^{\codelength}, \\ &\wit=\bff= (\bff_{*j})_{j\in [\codedim_2]}\in \FF_2^{\codedim_1 \times \codedim_2}\end{aligned}\right) \;\middle|\;  \begin{aligned} &f= \Enc_{\cC}\circ\pack(\bff) \\ \wedge \  &\prod_{i=1}^{\codedim_1} \left((\yy_i-1)\cdot \bff_{ij} +1\right) = \bm{\mu}_j  \text{ for all } j\in [\codedim_2]\end{aligned}  \right\}.
\end{align*}
%
We omit an explicit description of the polynomials $Q_{\mathbb{B}\text{it}\ZZ}$, since the fact that the above relation is a particular instance of the PESAT relation is not relevant in this paper.


\begin{comment}
\begin{remark}[Using interleaved codes] \albert{What is this remark for?} \label{r:interleaved_codes} In practice, one may use as code $\cC$ an $\codedim_2$ interleaving $\cC_1^{\equiv \codedim_2}$ of a code $\cC_1$ of dimension $\codedim_1/\comfieldexp$. Then, if $\comfieldexp$ divides $\codedim_1$ \albert{add as requirement somewhere},  then $$\pack\circ \picomfield(\ff)=(\pack\circ \picomfield(\ff_{*j}))_{j\in [\codedim_2]}$$
%
and 
 $$f = \Enc_{\cC_1^{\equiv \codedim_2}}\circ\pack\circ \picomfield(\bff) =  (\Enc_{\cC_1}\circ \pack\circ \picomfield(\bff_{*j}))_{j\in [\codedim_2]}\in \comfield^{\codelength}.$$
%
 %\albert{If we do interleacing, the reason why we work with an interleaved $\PESAT(\comfield, Q_{\mathbb{B}\text{it}\ZZ},\cC_1^{\equiv \codedim_2}, \pack)$ rather than $\codedim_2$ copies of a non-interleaved PESAT is rather technical. It has to do with keeping the same distance parameter when doing the reduction from $\LinBitsDual$ to $\LIN$. If we simply considered separate encodings of $\pack(\bff_{*j})$, the proximity parameter in from $\LIN$ to $\LinBitsDual$ would grow by a factor of $\codedim_2$.}
%
\end{remark}
\end{comment}
   

%
We assume prover and verifier have access to an IOR $\Pi_{\mathsf{red}}$  that reduces 
%
$
\PESAT(\comfield, Q_{\mathbb{B}\text{it}\ZZ},\cC, \pack)$ to $\LIN(\comfield, \cC, \pack)$, where, recall, this latter relation has predicates of the form $\langle \bfg, \uu\rangle = \mu'$ with $\bfg\in \FF_2^{\codedim}$, $\uu$ a vector over $\comfield$, and $g=\Enc_{\cC}\circ \pack(\bfg)$.   In our instantiation $\Pi_{\mathsf{red}}$ is an optimized GKR protocol for  grand products (cf.\ \cref{s:gkr_low_entropy}), which reduces the initial grand product claims to MLE evaluation claims of $\yy$ and $\bff$ over $\comfield$.

%
%
We assume $\Pi_{\mathsf{red}}$ works so that the verifier never queries $\oracle$; and that, given an input
%
$$\left(\inp=(\yy,\bm{\mu}),\  \oracle= f, \ \wit=\picomfield(\bff)\right)\in \PESAT(\comfield, Q_{\mathbb{B}\text{it}\ZZ},\cC, \pack),$$
%
$\Pi_{\mathsf{red}}$ outputs
%
$$
\left((\vv, \mu'), \ f, \ \picomfield(\bff)\right) \in \LIN( \comfield,\cC,\pack)$$ for some vector $\vv$ and field element $\mu'$. In particular, $\Pi_{\red}$ outputs the same oracle $f$ and witness $\pi_2(\bff)$ as the oracle and witness it is given as inputs. 


This is indeed the case with GKR: GKR reduces the initial circuit evaluation claim with a witness input, into an MLE evaluation claim of the same witness, without querying any oracle in-between. 





%
%\john{I actually don't like this name. There's too many things in the protocol that is linear and it's too vague}





\subsection{The {\ftwoz} IOP for the core Lin-BitsRing relation}


\paragraph{Algebraic set-up}


 Recall (\cref{s:prelims_algebra}) that all rings in this work are finitely generated as rings. We fix generators $\gamma_1, \ldots, \gamma_{k}\in R$ and we let
%
$$
\picanon^{R} : \ZZ[X_1,\ldots, X_{k}] \to R
$$
be the surjective ring homomorphism mapping each integer $a$ to $a\cdot 1\in R$, and  $X_i$ to $\gamma_{i}$ for $i\in [k]$. The choice of $\gamma_1,\ldots, \gamma_k$ is far from unique, see \cref{e:presentations} below.

We also fix an inverse map  $\picanon^{R}{}^{-1}: R \to \ZZ[X_1, \ldots, X_{k}]$ for $\picanon^{R}$, so that $\picanon^{R}\circ \picanon^{R}{}^{-1}=\mathsf{id}$. We refer to $\picanon^{R}{}^{-1}(a)$ as the \emph{lift} of $a\in R$. Note that, given $\picanon^R$, there may be many possible choices for $\picanon^{R}{}^{-1}$. In  \cref{e:presentations}  we provide example inverse maps.  

Recall (see \cref{s:prelims_algebra}) that $\pi_n$ denotes reduction modulo $n$ and that $\pi_n^{-1}$ maps each element from $\ZZ_n$ to its integer representative in $\range{0}{n-1}$.



%We also fix an inverse map $\picanon^{R}{}^{-1}: R \to \ZZ[X_1, \ldots, X_{\dim(R)-1}]$ of $\picanon^{R}$, defined as follows. Given $a\in R$, write $a=\sum_{i\in [\dim(R)]} a_i \cdot \gamma_i$ with $a_i\in \ZZ_{\Char(R)}$ (cf.\ \cref{s:prelims_algebra}), then $\picanon^{R}{}^{-1}(a) = \pi_{\Char}^{-1}(a_1) + \sum_{i=2}^{\dim(R)} \pi_{\Char}^{-1}(a_i)\cdot X_{i-1}$.


\begin{example}\label{e:presentations}
We provide some examples of rings $R$ and choices for  projections $\picanon^R$ and their inverses: 
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,nosep]
    \item \textbf{Prime fields.} If  $R=\FF_q$ is a prime field with $q$ elements, then we can take $k=0$, so that $\picanon^{\FF_q}=\pi_q:\ZZ \to \FF_q$ is simply reduction modulo $q$, with its inverse being $\pi_q^{-1}$ (see \cref{s:prelims_algebra}). 
    \item \textbf{Integers.} Similarly, for $R=\ZZ$ we can take $k=0$, and then $\picanon^{\ZZ}$ and its lifts are the identity map on $\ZZ$.
\item \textbf{Field extensions.} If $R$ is a field extension of a prime field $\FF_q$ of degree $d>1$, then we can  take $k=1$ and $\gamma_1 = x$, where $x$ is a root of an irreducible polynomial $h(X_1)\in \FF_{q}[X_1]$ of degree $d$ (so that $R=\FF_q(x)$). Then $\picanon^R:\ZZ[X_1]\to R$ consists in reducing coefficients modulo $q$, and then sending $X_1$ to $x$. Writing $a\in R$ as $a=\sum_{i=0}^{d-1} a_i\cdot x^{i}$ with $a_i\in \FF_q$, one can then define a natural lift of $a$ as $\sum_{i=0}^{d-1} \pi_q^{-1}(a_i)\cdot X_1^{i}$.
\item \textbf{Univariate integer polynomials.} If $R=\ZZ[X]$, then we can take $k=1$ and $\gamma_1=X$, and then $\picanon^{\ZZ[X]}:\ZZ[X_1]\to \ZZ[X]$ is an isomorphism, with lifts given by its inverse.
\item \textbf{Lattices, Galois rings, etc.} More generally, if $R$ is finitely generated as a $\ZZ_{\Char(R)}$-module and it is free as a $\ZZ_{\Char(R)}$-module, with  $\ZZ_{\Char(R)}$-module-basis $\beta_1=1, \beta_2, \ldots, \beta_m$ (a condition satisfied by, e.g., all finite fields, $\ZZ$, and $\ZZ_n[X]/(h(X))$ for any monic $h(X)$ and any integer $n\geq 0$), then we may take $k=m-1$ and $\gamma_i=\beta_{i+1}$ for $i\in [k]$. Then given $a\in R$ and writing $a=\sum_{i\in [m]} a_i\cdot \beta_i$ with $a_i\in \ZZ_{\Char(R)}$, we can defined the lift of $a$ as $\pi_{\Char}^{-1}(a_1) + \sum_{i=2}^{m}\pi_{\Char}^{-1}(a_i)\cdot X_{i-1}$.
\end{itemize}
\end{example}


We assume without further comment that it is efficient to compute  values $\picanon^R(h)$  for $h\in \ZZ[X_1,\ldots, X_k]$, and to check whether  $\picanon^R(h)=0$.   This is the case in all examples above.


\paragraph{The \ftwoz\ IOP.} We provide an IOP for $\LinBitsDual$ next. We assume the notation from the previous two \cref{s:linmodfieldbin,s:interleaved_setting}, and from the preliminary \cref{s:prelims_algebra}, together with:
\renewcommand{\evalfield}{R}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
%\item Given a finite field $\KK$ which is an extension of a prime field $\FF_p$ of degree $\degevalfield$, we let $\picanon^\KK: \ZZ[X] \to \KK$ be the canonical homomorphism from $\ZZ[X]$ to $\KK$: namely, if $\KK=\ZZ[X]/(p,h(X))$  for a polynomial $h(X)$ irreducible over $\FF_p[X]$, then $\picanon^\KK$ consists in modding $p$ out, and taking the quotient by $(h(X))$ (if $\KK$ is the prime field $\FF_p$, then $\picanon^\KK$ is just $\pi_p$). We fix an inverse for $\picanon^{\KK}$ of the form $$\picanon^{\KK}{}^{-1}: \KK \to \range{0}{p-1}^{<\degevalfield}[X] \subseteq \ZZ[X].
%$$ In words, $\picanon^{\KK}{}^{-1}$ simply takes an element from $\KK$ represented as a degree $< \deg(h(X))$ polynomial over $\FF_p$, and applies $\pi_{p}^{-1}$ to it. We remark that one could choose any other such ``inverse'' maps, e.g.\ for $\pi_{p}^{-1}$ one may prefer to use centered representatives. 





%\item Define $\picanon^\ZZ: \ZZ\to\ZZ$ as the identity map.  We define $\deg(\ZZ)=1$.



\item Let $\Pi_{\iopp}$ be an IOP for $\LIN(\comfield,\cC, \pack)$. In our instantiation, $\Pi_{\iopp}$ is the WHIR protocol \cite{whir} over binary fields, with ring-switching \cite{binius} as described in \cref{a:ring_switch_1}, and with proximity parameter set to the Johnson bound as specified in \cref{s:instantiation}.   
\item 
Let $\codedim =\codedim_1\cdot \codedim_2$ be dimension parameters,  and $R$ a ring with $k$ generators $\gamma_1,\ldots, \gamma_k$,  as in the previous sections. We let $\cP$ be a set of primes with $\max\cP< \fieldboundsizeexponent$. Given the tensor decomposition $\vv=\vv^{(1)}\otimes \vv^{(2)}$ from \cref{s:interleaved_setting}, we define \albert{Why a subscript $R$ in $N_R$ and not in the others?}
$$
\begin{aligned}
\uu &\defeq (\vv^{(1)},\vv^{(2)})\in R^{\codedim_1+ \codedim_2}\\
D &\defeq \max_{i\in [|\uu|]} \deg\left(\picanon^{R}{}^{-1}(\uu_i)\right), \qquad\qquad N_R \defeq \max_{i\in [|\uu|]}\ \left\lVert \picanon^{R}{}^{-1}(\uu_i)\right\rVert_\infty,
\end{aligned}
$$
%
where $\deg$ denotes total degree, and $\lVert \cdot \rVert_\infty$ denotes the maximum absolute value of the coefficients. We also define a quantity  \albert{Check} $$T\leq (D+1)^{k},$$ where $k$ is a minimum number of generators of $R$, as follows. $T$ is the maximum, over monomials $m$ in variables $X_1,\ldots, X_k$, of the number of pairs $(m_1, m_2)$ of monomials of total degree at most $D$ each such that $m_1\cdot m_2 = m$. 

For example, $T\leq D+1$ if $k=1$, and $T\leq 2$ if $D\leq 1$. In practice, for us, the bound $T\leq (D+1)^{k}$ is good enough.
%
%$$
%\Char^*(R) \defeq \begin{cases} \Char(R)-1 & \text{if } \Char(R)>0,\\ \max\left(\lVert \vv^{(1)}\rVert_\infty,\ \lVert \vv^{(2)}\rVert_\infty\right) & \text{if } R=\ZZ. \end{cases}
%$$

\item For every prime $q\in \cP$, let $\cM_q$ be a set of ring homomorphisms $$\pirand:\ZZ[X_1,\ldots, X_{k}] \to \FF_q.$$ The verifier will sample primes and morphisms from $\cP$ and $\cM_q$. We let $\theta(R,\codedim)$ be the maximum probability that $\pirand(h)=0$, over random $q\gets \cP$, $\pirand\gets \cM_q$, and where the maximum is taken over nonzero polynomials $h\in \ZZ[X_1, \ldots, X_{k}]$ of total degree at most $2\cdot D$, with $\lVert h\rVert_{\infty} \leq 2\cdot T\cdot \codedim\cdot N_R^2$, and such that $\picanon^{R}(h)\neq 0$. Precisely:
%
$$
\theta(R,\codedim) \defeq  \max_{h}\left\{\Pr_{q\gets \cP, \pirand\gets \cM_q}[ \pirand(h)=0] \right\},
$$
%
where $h$ ranges over the polynomials just described. See \cref{l:examples_morphisms} for an example.
\end{itemize}


\begin{lemma}[Example definition of  $\cM_q$ and the corresponding $\theta(R,\codedim)$]\label{l:examples_morphisms}
    Let $\cP$ be a set of primes. For each $q\in \cP$ let $\cM_q$  be the set of all maps $\pi: \ZZ[X_1,\ldots, X_{k}]\to \FF_q$ of the form
     $$\pi(h(X_1, \ldots, X_{k})) = \pi_q(h)(\alpha_1, \ldots, \alpha_{k}), \qquad \text{for } h\in \ZZ[X_1,\ldots, X_{k}],$$
%
for some $\alpha_1,\ldots, \alpha_{k}\in \FF_q$, and where $\pi_q(h)$ is $h$ after reducing its coefficients modulo $q$. Then \albert{Why do we have an $8$ here and before we had a $2$?}
         $$
    \theta(R,\codedim) \leq \frac{2\cdot D}{q_{\min}} + \frac{\log\left(8\cdot T\cdot \codedim\cdot N_R^2\right)}{|\cP|\cdot \lfloor\log(q_{\min})\rfloor}.
    $$
    where $q_{\min}$ is the smallest prime in $\cP$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    We defer the proof to later in \cref{s:soundness_core_iop}.
\end{proof}


\renewcommand{\degevalfield}{\deg(\evalfield)}
%\renewcommand{\picanon}{{\pi_{\mathsf{canon}}^R}}


%\albertimportant{This construction is missing OOD sample when we work beyond the UDR. Formalizing OOD sampling is a bit of a pain: we need to define a relation $\LinBitsDual^{\rr,y}$ which is the same as $\LinBitsDual$ but adds the constraint $\mle{\pack\circ\pi_2(\mathsf{\bits}(\bff))}(\rr) =y$ in $\comfield$. We do the same for $\UPESAT$. Then we present \cref{c:core_iop}  as a protocol for $\LinBitsDual^{\rr,y}$, and in \cref{c:LinModFieldBin} present an IOP for the general $\LinBitsDual$ relation where we first do an OOD sample, reducing to $\LinBitsDual^{\rr,y}$. In \cref{c:iop_pimsat} we also add an initial reduction from $\UPESAT$ to $\UPESAT^{\rr, y}$. All this is standard, so I am considering to write a short  section  glossing this over. The question is how to state the formal soundness results}

\begin{construction}[IOP for the core {$\LinBitsDual$ relation}]\label{c:core_iop}
\noindent \textbf{Parameters.} $(\codedim_1,\codedim_2, \Pi_\iopp, \Pi_\red, \cP, (\cM_q)_{q\in \cP})$ comprised of: tensor decomposition dimensions $\codedim_1, \codedim_2$  satisfying $\codedim_1\cdot \codedim_2 = \codedim$; the IOP $\Pi_\iopp$ described above, the IOR $\Pi_\red$ from \cref{s:?}; and the sets $\cP, \cM_q$ from above. \albert{Parameters changed. Change when calling this construction. I put as parameters everything that is used by the protocol that is not present in the definition of the relation already} 
\\

\noindent \textbf{Inputs.} The prover and verifier receive $\inp=(\mu, \vv)\in \evalfield \times \evalfield^{\codedim}$ and  $\oracle= f \in \comfield^\codelength$. The prover receives $\wit= \bff\in \bits^\codedim \subseteq \ZZ^\codedim$ such that $(\inp,\oracle, \wit) \in \LinBitsDual(\bits,\ringmorphism, \evalfield,\cC)$.  We assume the tensor split from the previous \cref{s:interleaved_setting}, and adopt its notation. 


Let $\basegenerator\in \comfield$ be a fixed generator of the multiplicative group $\comfield^*$. Denote $\picanon^R$ simply as $\picanon$.  %Let $q\in \ZZ$ denote the characteristic of $R$ if $R$ has nonzero characteristic, or if $R$ has zero characteristic, then \albertimportant{Review that if $\vv$ has negative entries, things are fine}
%\begin{equation}\label{e:q_definition_if_R_is_Z}
%q = \codedim_1 \cdot \lVert \vv \rVert_\infty +1.
%\end{equation}



\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*] 
\item \textbf{Round 0 (optional): Out Of Domain (OOD) sample.} This step can be skipped if the proximity parameter $\delta$ is  set within the unique decoding radius of $\cC$ (cf.\ \cref{s:prelims_codes}, and \cref{t:thm_core_IOPP}). Otherwise, the verifier samples and sends a random $\zeta\in \comfield$, and the prover responds with $$y=\mle{\pack\circ\pi_2(\bff)}(\bm{\zeta}), \qquad \text{where } \bm{\zeta}\defeq \left(\zeta^{2^0}, \zeta^{2^1}, \ldots, \zeta^{2^{\log (\codedim/\comfieldexp) -1}}\right).$$
\item \textbf{Round 1 (optional): replacing $R$ by a ``small'' prime field $\FF_q$.}
\albertimportant{Round numbering has changed}
This step can be skipped (i.e.\ prover and verifier move to Round 2 directly) if $R$ is a prime field $\FF_q$ and $q < \fieldboundsizeexponent$. %, or if $R=\ZZ$ and $\lVert \vv \rVert < \fieldboundsizeexponent$.

The prover sends $\mu' \in \ZZ[X_1, \ldots, X_{k}]$ such that
%
$$
\langle \picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(1)}) \otimes \picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(2)}) , \bff \rangle = \mu' \qquad \text{in } \ZZ[X_1, \ldots, X_{k}].
$$
%
The verifier checks that $\mu'$ has total degree at most $2\cdot D$ and coefficients in $\range{-T\cdot \codedim\cdot N_R^2}{T\cdot \codedim\cdot N_R^2}$ \albert{state with the infinity norm}, checks that $\picanon(\mu')=\mu$, and replies with a random prime $q\in \cP$ and a random morphism
%
$
\pirand : \ZZ[X_1, \ldots, X_{k}] \to \FF_q
$ from $\cM_q$.
 The prover and verifier update:
\begin{align*}
\vv^{(1)}{} &\gets \pirand\circ \picanon^{-1}\left(\vv^{(1)}\right)\in \FF_{q}^{\codedim_1},\\
\vv^{(2)}{} &\gets \pirand\circ \picanon^{-1}\left(\vv^{(2)}\right)\in \FF_{q}^{\codedim_2},\\
\mu&\gets \pirand(\mu')\in \FF_q,
\end{align*}
The prover and verifier now set to prove the updated claim 
%
\begin{equation}\label{e:core_bitz_eq_new_0}
\langle \vv^{(1)} \otimes \vv^{(2)}, \pi_q(\bff)\rangle = \mu \qquad \text{over } \FF_q.
\end{equation}

\begin{remark}[Skipping Round 1 when $R=\ZZ$ and $\lVert \vv\rVert_{\infty}$ is ``small'']\label{r:skip_round_1_integers}
\albert{Make sure our completeness and soundness theorem proves this case as well}
    This step can also be skipped when $R=\ZZ$ and all entries of $\vv^{(1)}$ are nonnegative and less than $\fieldboundsizeexponent$. In that case the claim to prove at the start of Round 2 is $\langle \vv^{(1)} \otimes \vv^{(2)}, \bff\rangle = \mu $ over $\ZZ$. In this case the map $\pi_q$ in Round 2 is simply replaced by the identity map, and $\FF_q$ is replaced by $\ZZ$. In Phase 3, $\bm{\gamma}$ is  $\vv^{(1)}$. 
\end{remark}
\item \textbf{Round 2: transforming \eqref{e:core_bitz_eq_new_0} to $\codedim_2$ integer constraints with bounded integers.}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
    \item The prover sends integers $\mu_1, \ldots, \mu_{\codedim_2}\in \range{0}{(q-1)\cdot \codedim_1}$ such that, over $\ZZ$, \begin{equation}\label{e:tensored_equality}\langle \bff_{*j}, \pi_q^{-1}(\vv^{(1)})\rangle = \mu_j \qquad \text{for all } j\in [\codedim_2].\end{equation} 
\item The verifier checks that $\mu_j\in \range{0}{(q-1)\cdot \codedim_1}$ for all $j\in [\codedim_2]$, and that \begin{equation}\label{e:tensor_decomposition_reconstruction}\sum_{j\in [\codedim_2]} \pi_q(\mu_j)\cdot \vv_j^{(2)}  = \mu\qquad \text{over } \FF_q.\end{equation} 
Next, prover and verifier will focus on certifying that \eqref{e:tensored_equality} holds for all $j\in [\codedim_2]$.

In \cref{r:core_iop_optional_sumcheck} we describe a variation in which, instead of sending the elements $\mu_1, \ldots, \mu_{\codedim_2}$, the prover commits to their bit-decomposition over $\comfield$, and proves \eqref{e:tensor_decomposition_reconstruction} with \cref{c:LinModFieldBin}. This saves on proof size and verifier time when $\codedim$ is large.
%\item \textbf{Optional step: Projecting the integer claim to a prime field, and then lifting it back up to $\ZZ$.} The following step triggers in the following scenarios:
%\begin{itemize}
%    \item $\degevalfield>1$.
%    \item $\degevalfield=1$ and $q\geq (|\comfield|-1)/(\codedim_1+1)$.  \albert{Review if I need the $+1$ in the denominator}
%\end{itemize} The verifier uniformly samples and sends a  prime $q'$ from $\cP$, and, if $\degevalfield>1$,  an element $\alpha\in \FF_{q'}$. Now update
%
%\begin{align*}
%\vv^{(1)}{} &\gets \pi_{q',\alpha}\circ \picanon^{-1}\left(\vv^{(1)}\right)\in \FF_{q'}^{\codedim_1},\\
%\mu_j &\gets \pi_{q'}^{-1}\circ \pi_{q',\alpha}(\mu_j) \in \range{0}{q'-1}\subseteq \ZZ, \qquad j\in [\codedim_2],\\
%q&\gets q',
%\end{align*}
%where, recall, $\pi_{q',\alpha}:\ZZ[X]\to \FF_{q'}$ is defined as coefficient-wise reduction modulo $q'$ and then evaluation of $X$ at $\alpha$.
%\end{itemize}
%
%\begin{remark}
%[Using any module homomorphism instead of $\pi_{q',\alpha}$]\label{r:otheSodule_morphisms}
%Instead of sampling $\pi_{q',\alpha}$, the verifier could sample other $\ZZ$-module homomorphisms from $\ZZ^{<\mathsf{deg}(R)}[X]$ to a prime field. For example it could sample a prime $q'$ and $\mathsf{deg}(R)$ random elements $\alpha_1,\ldots, \alpha_{\mathsf{deg}(R)}\in \FF_{q'}$, and then prover and verifier use the map $\pi_{q',\alpha_1,\ldots, \alpha_{\mathsf{deg}(R)}}(f(X)) = \sum_{i\in [\mathsf{deg}(R)]}\pi_{q'}(f_{i}) \cdot \alpha_i$, where $f(X)=\sum_{i\in [\mathsf{deg}(R)]} f_{i}\cdot X^{i-1}$. 
%$$
%For such a class of maps, the error term $(\mathsf{deg}(R)-1)/q_{\min}$ of \cref{t:thm_core_IOPP} becomes $1/q_{\min}$.
%\end{remark}

%
%For ease of readability onwards, we use the same symbols $\vv^{(1)}$ and $q$ for the pre- and post-update vectors and primes in this step.
\end{itemize}
\item \textbf{Phase 3: Transforming the claim into a PESAT claim over {$\comfield$}, and invoking the IOR {$\Pi_{\mathsf{red}}$}.}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
\item 
Prover and verifier define $$\bm{\gamma} = \pi_{q}^{-1}(\vv^{(1)}) \in \range{0}{q-1}^{\codedim_1}\subseteq \ZZ^{\codedim_1},$$
%
and set to prove the following claim:
%
$$
\prod_{i \in [\codedim_1]} g^{\bm{\gamma}_i\cdot \bff_{ij}}  = g^{\mu_j} \in \comfield, \qquad \text{for all } j\in [\codedim_2].
$$
This claim is equivalent to
$$
\prod_{i \in [\codedim_1]} \left(\left(g^{\bm{\gamma}_i}-1\right)\cdot \pi_2(\bff_{ij}) +1\right)= g^{\mu_j}  \quad  \in \comfield, \qquad \text{for all } j\in [\codedim_2].
$$
Notice that \begin{equation}\label{e:Q_F2Z_intermediate}\left(\inp=((g^{\bm{\gamma}_i})_{i \in [\codedim_1]},\ (g^{\mu_j})_{j\in [\codedim_2]}),\  \oracle= f, \ \wit=\picomfield(\bff)\right)\in \PESAT(\comfield, Q_{\mathbb{B}\text{it}\ZZ},\cC, \pack).
\end{equation}
% \ulrich{%
% Suggestion: 
% Maybe it is more clear, if we write in the above two equalities explicitly a $\bmod\: q$, or, add $\in \mathbb F_q$?
% }%
% \albert{See now. It's actually in $\comfield$}
\item To prove \eqref{e:Q_F2Z_intermediate}, prover and verifier engage in the IOR $\Pi_{\mathsf{red}}$\footnote{Recall that, in our instantiation, $\Pi_{\mathsf{red}}$ is the GKR protocol applied to multiple grand products, cf.\ \cref{s:gkr_low_entropy}.}   with input $$\left(\inp=((g^{\bm{\gamma}_i})_{i \in [\codedim_1]},\ (g^{\mu_j})_{j\in [\codedim_2]}),\  \oracle= f, \ \wit=\picomfield(\bff)\right).$$
%
%
 At the end of this IOR, they are left with the following claim to prove: 
%
\begin{equation}\label{e:iopp_mle_claims}
\left((\vv, \mu'), \ f, \ \picomfield(\bff)\right) \in \LIN( \comfield,\cC,\pack)= \left\{ \left(\begin{aligned} &\inp=(\uu,\mu)\in \comfield^{\codedim}\times \comfield, \\&\oracle = g \in \comfield^{\codelength}, \\ &\wit= \bfg\in \FF_2^\codedim\end{aligned}\right) \;\middle|\;  \begin{aligned} 
&g=\Enc_{\cC}\circ \pack (\bfg) \\    &\langle \uu, \bfg\rangle = \mu  \end{aligned}\right\}
\end{equation} for some vector $\vv$ and field element $\mu'$ output during the IOR $\Pi_{\mathsf{red}}$. 
\end{itemize}
\item \textbf{Phase 4: Using an IOP over {$\comfield$}.} Prover and  verifier engage in the IOP $\Pi_{\iopp}$  to certify the claim \eqref{e:iopp_mle_claims}.  If Round 0 (Out Of Domain sampling) triggered, they also use $\Pi_{\iopp}$ to certify the evaluation claim from Round 0.
\end{enumerate}
\end{construction}


\begin{remark}[Reducing proof size by committing to the elements $\mu_1, \ldots, \mu_{\codedim_2}$]  \label{r:core_iop_optional_sumcheck}
When $\codedim=\codedim_1\cdot \codedim_2$ is such that $\codedim_2$ is large, the elements $\mu_1,\ldots, \mu_{\codedim_2}\in \ZZ$ sent at Round 2 of \cref{c:core_iop} may start to dominate the proof size. For example, for $\codedim=2^{13}$ and $\mu_i$ of $128$ bits, the elements $\mu_1,\ldots, \mu_{\codedim_2}$ amount to $131$ KB.  To mitigate this cost,  one can have the prover commit to the vector $(\mu_1,\ldots, \mu_{\codedim_2})$ and then prove \eqref{e:tensor_decomposition_reconstruction}, i.e.\ 
%
$$
\langle \bff_{*j}, \pi_q^{-1}(\vv^{(1)})\rangle = \mu_j \qquad \text{for all } j\in [\codedim_2].
$$
%
using an IOP for constrained codes. Concretely, the prover commits to the vector $\bitstext(\mu)$ of bits forming the elements $(\mu_1, \ldots, \mu_{\codedim_2})$ using \ftwoz, and then proves \eqref{e:tensor_decomposition_reconstruction} using \cref{c:LinModFieldBin} from \cref{s: general_bitz}.   %At the end of \cref{c:core_iop}, the prover is left with an inner product claim for the vector $\pi_2(\bitstext(\mu))$.  
%
\end{remark}


\subsection{Soundness and completeness}\label{s:soundness_core_iop}


For readability, we temporarily denote $$\PESAT=\PESAT(\comfield, Q_{\mathbb{B}\text{it}\ZZ},\cC, \pack),\qquad \LIN = \LIN(\comfield, \cC, \pack).$$




\begin{theorem}[Completeness and round-by-round knowledge soundness of \cref{c:core_iop}]\label{t:thm_core_IOPP} 
   Let $\delta \in (0,1)$ be a proximity parameter, and let $L_\delta\geq 1$ be such that $\cC$ is $(\delta, L_\delta)$-list-decodable (cf.\ \cref{s:prelims_codes}).  Assume 
   %
   $$\max\cP < \fieldboundsizeexponent.$$ 
   %
   Assume $\Pi_{\mathsf{red}}$ is a perfectly complete $t_\red$-round IOR from $\PESAT$ to $\LIN$ with round-by-round knowledge soundness  with relaxation $(\PESAT^\delta,\LIN^{\delta})$, with errors  $(\ksred_1, \ldots, \ksred_{t_\red})$. Assume $\Pi_{\iopp}$ is a perfectly complete $t_{\iopp}$-round IOP  for $\LIN$ (i.e.\ an IOR from $\LIN$ to the trivial relation $\bot$) with round-by-round knowledge soundness with relaxation $(\LIN^{\delta}, \bot)$, and errors $$\ksiopp_1,\ldots, \ksiopp_{t_\iopp}.$$ 
%
    Then \cref{c:core_iop} is a perfectly complete $(2+t_\red+t_\iopp)$-round IOP for $\LinBitsDual$ with round-by-round knowledge soundness with relaxation $(\LinBitsDual^{\delta}, \bot)$, with errors $$ \kappa_{\mathsf{OOD}}, \kappa_0, (\ksred_i)_{i\in [t_\red]}, (\ksiopp_i)_{i\in [t_\iopp]},$$ where  %\albertimportant{Make sure the Instantiation section accounts for the OOD term}
    %
        $$
    \kappa_{\mathsf{OOD}} \leq \binom{L_\delta}{2}\cdot \frac{\codedim/\comfieldexp-1}{|\comfield|}, \qquad \kappa_0 = \theta(R,\codedim)
    $$
    (see \cref{l:ood_collision,l:examples_morphisms}).
    The error terms $\kappa_{\mathsf{OOD}}, \kappa_0$ are void if either Round 0 or Round 1 are not executed, respectively. In that case \cref{c:core_iop} has one or two rounds less.
    %Recall that $q$ is either the characteristic of $R$ if $R$ is a finite field, or $q=\codedim_1 \cdot \lVert \vv\rVert_{\infty} +1$ if $R=\ZZ$.
%
%
    %If  $\evalfield$ is a prime field, then \cref{c:core_iop} is a $t_\red+t_\iopp$-round IOP with round-by-round knowledge soundness with relaxation $(\LinBitsDual^\delta, \bot)$, with errors $(\ksred_i)_{i\in [t_\red]}, (\ksiopp_i)_{i\in [t_\iopp]}$.
\end{theorem}




 To prove \cref{l:examples_morphisms} we will use the following result, which generalizes \albert{point to lemma} in \cite{zincplus}.
\begin{lemma}\label{l:reduction_lemma}
Let $h\in \ZZ[X_1, \ldots, X_m]$ be a nonzero polynomial of total degree $\leq d$ and with coefficients in the range $\range{-2^{B}+1}{2^B-1}$. Let $\cP$ be a set of primes, and let $q_{\min}$ be the smallest prime in $\cP$. Given a prime $q$ and $\bm{\alpha}\in \FF_q^m$, write $\pi_{q,\bm{\alpha}}(h)\defeq \pi_q(h)(\bm{\alpha})\in \FF_q$. Then \albert{should we have two cases in the second term? One with denom $1$ and another with denom $d$?}
%
$$
\Pr_{\substack{q\gets \cP,\\\bm{\alpha} \gets \FF_q^m}}[\pi_{q,\bm{\alpha}}(h)=0] \leq \frac{B}{\lfloor\log(q_{\min})\rfloor\cdot |\cP|} + \frac{d}{q_{\min}}.
$$
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Let $\cE_{\mathsf{prime}}$ be the event that all coefficients of $h$ are  multiples of $q$. Then, by the Schwartz--Zippel lemma, $$\Pr_{\substack{q\gets \cP,\\\bm{\alpha} \gets \FF_q^m}}[\pi_{q,\bm{\alpha}}(h)=0\mid \neg \cE_{\mathsf{prime}}] \leq  \frac{d}{q_{\min}}.$$
%
Now, every nonzero coefficient of $h$ is divisible by at most $B/\lfloor\log(q_{\min})\rfloor$ primes from $\cP$. Hence, the probability that $\cE_{\mathsf{prime}}$ occurs is at most $(B/\lfloor\log(q_{\min})\rfloor)/|\cP|$, and the lemma follows.
%
\end{proof}



\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{l:examples_morphisms}]
Let $h\in \ZZ[X_1,\ldots, X_{k}]$ be a polynomial of total degree at most $2\cdot D$, with coefficients in $\range{-2\cdot T\cdot \codedim\cdot N_R^2}{2\cdot T\cdot \codedim\cdot N_R^2}$, and with $\picanon^{R}(h)\neq 0$ (cf.\ the definition of $\theta(R,\codedim)$). In particular $h$ is nonzero, and its coefficients lie in $\range{-2^{B}+1}{2^{B}-1}$ for $B\defeq \lfloor \log(T\cdot \codedim\cdot N_R^2)\rfloor + 2 \leq \log(8\cdot T\cdot \codedim\cdot N_R^2)$. Since $h$ is nonzero and has total degree at most $2\cdot D$, \cref{l:reduction_lemma} (applied with $m=k$ variables and total degree $2\cdot D$) yields $$\Pr_{\substack{q\gets \cP,\\ \bm{\alpha} \gets \FF_q^{k}}}[\pi_q(h)(\bm{\alpha})=0] \leq \frac{B}{\lfloor\log(q_{\min})\rfloor\cdot |\cP|} + \frac{2\cdot D}{q_{\min}},$$ as needed.
%
\end{proof}


%The following lemma bounds the probability, over the OOD challenge $\zeta$ of Round 0, that two distinct bit vectors close to the oracle share the same OOD evaluation. Throughout, for $\zeta\in \comfield$ we write, as in Round 0 of \cref{c:core_iop}, $$\bm{\zeta}= \left(\zeta^{2^0}, \zeta^{2^1}, \ldots, \zeta^{2^{\log(\codedim/\comfieldexp)-1}}\right)\in \comfield^{\log (\codedim/\comfieldexp)}$$ for the corresponding evaluation point.


\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{t:thm_core_IOPP}] 
%
The completeness of \cref{c:core_iop} is clear from its description \albert{Review}.  To prove its soundness, we first treat the case where Round 0 is not executed, so that, by hypothesis, $L_\delta=1$; the case where Round 0 is executed is treated at the end of the proof.  We decompose  \cref{c:core_iop} into three IORs $\Pi_1, \Pi_2, \Pi_3$.  The first one, $\Pi_1$, is an IOR from $\LinBitsDual$ to $\PESAT$, and it consists of applying Rounds 1 and 2 of \cref{c:core_iop}, and outputting $$\left(\inp'=((g^{\bm{\gamma}_i})_{i \in [\codedim_1]},\ (g^{\mu_j})_{j\in [\codedim_2]}),\  \oracle'= f, \ \wit'=\picomfield(\bff)\right),$$ as described in Phase 3 of \cref{c:core_iop}. Formally, $\Pi_1$ is a $2$-round IOR whose second verifier message is empty: the prover first sends $\mu'$, the verifier replies with $(q,\pirand)$, and the prover then sends $\bm{\mu}=(\mu_1,\ldots, \mu_{\codedim_2})$. The second IOR, $\Pi_2$, is simply $\Pi_{\mathsf{red}}$, reducing $\PESAT$ to $\LIN$. Finally, the third IOR, $\Pi_3$, is simply the IOP $\Pi_{\iopp}$ for $\LIN$.



\albert{Review this paragraph.} Below we show that $\Pi_1$  has round-by-round knowledge soundness with relaxation $(\LinBitsDual^{\delta}, \PESAT^{\delta})$, with errors $(\kappa_0, 0)$. The second verifier message of $\Pi_1$ is empty, hence the last prover message of $\Pi_1$ can be sent together with the first prover message of $\Pi_2$, and thus  \cref{c:core_iop} has $1+t_\red+t_\iopp$ rounds. The theorem then follows from the composition of IORs result (cf.\ \cref{r:ior_compositoin}). If Round 1 is skipped, then the messages $\mu'$ and $(q,\pirand)$ and the error $\kappa_0$ disappear, and the arguments below apply after replacing $\pirand\circ \picanon^{-1}$ with the identity map on $\FF_q$ or on $\ZZ$. In the latter case,  $\pi_q$ is also replaced by the identity map, cf.\ \cref{r:skip_round_1_integers}.




\paragraph{Completeness and soundness of $\Pi_1$}
We describe a knowledge state function $\kstate$. Let $\inp=(\mu,\vv)\in \evalfield \times \evalfield^{\codedim}$ and  $\oracle=f\in \comfield^{\codelength}$. Let $\tr$ be a partial interaction transcript for $\Pi_1$. We start by setting $\kstate$ so that the first condition in \cref{def:knowledge-state-function} is met, and so that the second one is met at the prover message $\mu'$. Precisely, if   $\tr=\emptyset$, we set $\kstate(\inp,\oracle,\tr,\ww) = 1$ if and only if $(\inp, \oracle, \ww)\in \LinBitsDual^{\delta}$; and, if $\tr=(\mu')$ consists of a single prover message, we set $\kstate(\inp,\oracle,(\mu'),\ww)=\kstate(\inp,\oracle,\emptyset,\ww)$.

After the verifier's message, $\tr$ is as follows:
%
$$
\tr=(\mu', (q,\pirand)) \in \ZZ[X_1, \ldots, X_{k}] \times \left(\cP \times \cM_q\right).
$$
%
We define $\kstate(\inp,\oracle,\tr,\ww)=1$ if and only if  the verifier's degree and range checks on $\mu'$ in Round 1 pass,  $\ww=\bff\in \bits^{\codedim}\subseteq \ZZ^\codedim$,  $\bff=(\bff_{*j})_{j\in [\codedim_2]}\in \left(\bits^{\codedim_1}\right)^{\codedim_2}$, and
%
\begin{equation}\label{e:core_iop_proof_eq1}
    \begin{aligned}
&\picanon(\mu')=\mu,\\
&\langle \pirand\circ \picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(1)}) \otimes \pirand\circ \picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(2)}), \pi_q(\bff)\rangle = \pirand(\mu') \quad \text{ over } \FF_q,\\
&\hamming(f,\Enc_{\cC}\circ\pack\circ \pi_2(\bff))< \delta.
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
%
Next, let $\tr=(\mu', (q,\pirand), \bm{\mu})$ for a prover message $\bm{\mu}=(\mu_1,\ldots, \mu_{\codedim_2})\in \range{0}{(q-1)\cdot \codedim_1}^{\codedim_2}$, or let $\tr$ be the full transcript obtained by appending the empty second verifier message to it (this appending does not change the value of $\kstate$ below, and hence the second round of $\Pi_1$ has round-by-round knowledge error $0$). We set $\kstate(\inp,\oracle,\tr,\ww)=1$ if and only if the verifier accepts on $\tr$ (i.e.\ its checks in Rounds 1 and 2 pass) and $(\inp',\oracle',\ww)\in \PESAT^{\delta}$, where $(\inp',\oracle')$ is the output of the verifier, namely
%
$$
\inp'=((g^{\bm{\gamma}_i})_{i \in [\codedim_1]},\ (g^{\mu_j})_{j\in [\codedim_2]}), \qquad \oracle'= f, \qquad \text{with } \bm{\gamma} = \pi_{q}^{-1}\left(\pirand\circ \picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(1)})\right) \in \range{0}{q-1}^{\codedim_1}\subseteq \ZZ^{\codedim_1}
$$
%
(here and below we identify $\ww=\bff\in \bits^\codedim\subseteq \ZZ^{\codedim}$ with $\picomfield(\bff)\in \FF_2^{\codedim}$). Thus $\kstate$ satisfies the third condition in \cref{def:knowledge-state-function}.



We define $\rbrextractor(\inp,\oracle,\tr,\ww)=\ww$. It remains to prove two statements: first, that
%
\[
  \Pr_{q,\pirand}\!\left[
  \exists\,\ww \ \left| \ \begin{array}{l}
  \kstate\bigl(\inp,\oracle,(\mu'),\rbrextractor(\inp,\oracle,(\mu', (q,\pirand)),\ww)\bigr)=0\\
  \wedge\ \kstate(\inp,\oracle,(\mu', (q,\pirand)), \ww)=1
  \end{array}
  \right.\right]
  \ \le\ \kappa_0;
\]
and second, that $\kstate$ satisfies the second condition in \cref{def:knowledge-state-function} for transcripts where the prover is about to send $\bm{\mu}$.

\bigskip

We start with the first statement. Assume $$\kstate(\inp,\oracle,(\mu', (q,\pirand)), \ww)=1 \quad \text{and} \quad \kstate(\inp,\oracle,(\mu'),  \ww)=\kstate(\inp,\oracle,\emptyset,\ww)=0.$$
The latter occurs if and only if  $(\inp,\oracle,\ww)\notin \LinBitsDual^{\delta}$. The former implies that $\ww=\bff=(\bff_{*j})_{j\in[\codedim_2]}\in \bits^\codedim$ satisfies \eqref{e:core_iop_proof_eq1}; in particular $\hamming(f,\Enc_{\cC}\circ\pack\circ \pi_2(\bff))< \delta$, and hence, since $(\inp,\oracle,\ww)\notin \LinBitsDual^{\delta}$, necessarily
%
\begin{equation}\label{e:break_soundness_2}
\langle \pi(\bff), \vv\rangle \neq \mu \qquad \text{over } R.
\end{equation}
%
Define $$h\defeq  \langle \picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(1)}) \otimes \picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(2)}), \bff \rangle- \mu' \ \in \ \ZZ[X_1, \ldots, X_{k}].
$$
Since $\picanon$ is a ring homomorphism satisfying $\picanon\circ \picanon^{-1}=\mathsf{id}$ and restricting to $\pi$ on $\ZZ$, the first condition in \eqref{e:core_iop_proof_eq1} gives $$\picanon(h) = \langle \pi(\bff), \vv^{(1)} \otimes \vv^{(2)} \rangle- \picanon(\mu') = \langle \pi(\bff), \vv\rangle - \mu,$$ which is nonzero by \eqref{e:break_soundness_2}. In particular $h\neq 0$. On the other hand, since $\pirand$ is a ring homomorphism restricting to $\pi_q$ on $\ZZ$, the second condition in \eqref{e:core_iop_proof_eq1} states precisely that $\pirand(h)=0$. Moreover, $h$ has total degree at most $2\cdot D$ and coefficients in $\range{-2\cdot T\cdot \codedim\cdot N_R^2}{2\cdot T\cdot \codedim\cdot N_R^2}$: indeed, the lifts $\picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(1)}_i)$ and $\picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(2)}_j)$ have total degree at most $D$ and coefficients of absolute value at most $N_R$,

Since $\picanon$ is a ring homomorphism satisfying $\picanon\circ \picanon^{-1}=\mathsf{id}$ and restricting to $\pi$ on $\ZZ$, the first condition in \eqref{e:core_iop_proof_eq1} gives $$\picanon(h) = \langle \pi(\bff), \vv^{(1)} \otimes \vv^{(2)} \rangle- \picanon(\mu') = \langle \pi(\bff), \vv\rangle - \mu,$$ which is nonzero by \eqref{e:break_soundness_2}. In particular $h\neq 0$. On the other hand, since $\pirand$ is a ring homomorphism restricting to $\pi_q$ on $\ZZ$, the second condition in \eqref{e:core_iop_proof_eq1} states precisely that $\pirand(h)=0$. Moreover, $h$ has total degree at most $2\cdot D$ and coefficients in $\range{-2\cdot T\cdot \codedim\cdot N_R^2}{2\cdot T\cdot \codedim\cdot N_R^2}$: indeed, the lifts $\picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(1)}_i)$ and $\picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(2)}_j)$ have total degree at most $D$ and coefficients of absolute value at most $N_R$, so each product $\picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(1)}_i)\cdot \picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(2)}_j)$ has total degree at most $2\cdot D$ and coefficients of absolute value at most $T\cdot N_R^2$ (by the definition of $T$); the entries of $\bff$ are bits, and $\mu'$ passes the verifier's degree and range checks of Round 1. Since additionally $\picanon(h)\neq 0$, the polynomial $h$ belongs to the class of polynomials over which $\theta(R,\codedim)$ is defined. Finally,  since we have assumed for now that  $\cC$ is $(\delta,1)$-list-decodable, there is at most one $\ww$ such that \ $\hamming(f,\Enc_{\cC}\circ\pack\circ \pi_2(\ww))< \delta$.  Therefore  \albert{review} $$
\begin{aligned}
&\Pr_{q,\pirand}[\exists \ww  \text{ such that } (\inp,\oracle,\ww)\notin \LinBitsDual^\delta, \text{ and }\ww=\bff \text{ satisfies  \eqref{e:core_iop_proof_eq1}}]\\ \leq &\Pr_{q\gets \cP,\ \pirand\gets \cM_q}[\pirand(h)=0] \leq \theta(R,\codedim) = \kappa_0,
\end{aligned}
$$
%
where the last inequality holds by the definition of $\theta(R,\codedim)$.

\bigskip

It remains to prove the second statement, namely: if $\kstate(\inp,\oracle,(\mu', (q,\pirand)),\ww)=0$, then $\kstate(\inp,\oracle,(\mu', (q,\pirand), \bm{\mu}),\ww)=0$ for every prover message $\bm{\mu}=(\mu_1,\ldots, \mu_{\codedim_2})\in \range{0}{(q-1)\cdot \codedim_1}^{\codedim_2}$. Suppose towards a contradiction that $\kstate(\inp,\oracle,(\mu', (q,\pirand), \bm{\mu}),\ww)=1$, i.e.\ that the verifier accepts and $(\inp',\oracle',\ww)\in \PESAT^{\delta}$. Then $\ww=\bff=(\bff_{*j})_{j\in [\codedim_2]}\in \bits^\codedim$, $\hamming( f,\Enc_{\cC}\circ \pack\circ \pi_2(\bff)) < \delta$, and, for all $j\in [\codedim_2]$, $$g^{\langle \bff_{*j}, \bm{\gamma}\rangle} = \prod_{i \in [\codedim_1]} \left(\left(g^{\bm{\gamma}_i}-1\right)\cdot \pi_2(\bff_{ij}) +1\right)= g^{\mu_j},$$ and hence $$\langle \bff_{*j}, \bm{\gamma}\rangle = \mu_j \mod \mathsf{ord}(g),$$ where $\mathsf{ord}(g)$ denotes the multiplicative order of $g$. Now, since $\bm{\gamma}_i\in \range{0}{q-1}$, $\mu_j\in \range{0}{(q-1)\cdot \codedim_1}$, and $\bff_{*j}$ contains bits, we have $|\langle \bff_{*j}, \bm{\gamma}\rangle - \mu_j| \leq \codedim_1 \cdot (q-1)$. Since $g$ is a generator of $\comfield^*$ and $q\leq \max\cP< \fieldboundsizeexponent$, we have $$\mathsf{ord}(g) = |\comfield|-1> \codedim_1\cdot \max\cP \geq \codedim_1\cdot q> \codedim_1 \cdot (q-1),$$ and therefore
%
\begin{equation}\label{e:mu_j_integer_equalities}
\langle \bff_{*j}, \bm{\gamma}\rangle = \mu_j \qquad \text{over } \ZZ, \qquad \text{ for all } j\in [\codedim_2].
\end{equation}
%
Applying $\pi_q$ to \eqref{e:mu_j_integer_equalities}, and using that $\pi_q(\bm{\gamma})=\pirand\circ \picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(1)})$, we obtain $$\langle \pi_q(\bff_{*j}), \pirand\circ \picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(1)})\rangle = \pi_q(\mu_j) \quad \text{ over } \FF_q, \qquad \text{ for all } j\in [\codedim_2].$$ Since the verifier accepts, its check \eqref{e:tensor_decomposition_reconstruction} in Round 2 passes, i.e.\ $\sum_{j\in [\codedim_2]} \pi_q(\mu_j)\cdot \left(\pirand\circ \picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(2)})\right)_j = \pirand(\mu')$ (recall that $\vv^{(2)}$ and $\mu$ were updated in Round 1). Combining,
%
\begin{align*}
\langle \pirand\circ \picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(1)}) \otimes \pirand\circ \picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(2)}), \pi_q(\bff)\rangle &= \sum_{j\in [\codedim_2]} \langle \pi_q(\bff_{*j}), \pirand\circ \picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(1)})\rangle \cdot \left(\pirand\circ \picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(2)})\right)_j \\ &= \sum_{j\in [\codedim_2]} \pi_q(\mu_j)\cdot \left(\pirand\circ \picanon^{-1}(\vv^{(2)})\right)_j = \pirand(\mu').
\end{align*}
%
That is, the second condition in \eqref{e:core_iop_proof_eq1} holds. The first condition in \eqref{e:core_iop_proof_eq1} holds because the verifier accepts, and the third one was noted above. Hence $\kstate(\inp,\oracle,(\mu', (q,\pirand)),\ww)=1$, a contradiction.



\paragraph{The case where Round 0 is executed} In \cref{?} we treat the case when Round 0 is executed, so that $\cC$ is $(\delta, L_\delta)$-list-decodable, possibly with $L_\delta>1$. The arguments are standard, being essentially those from \cite{stir,whir}.
%
\end{proof}





\subsection{The general Lin-BitsRing relation}\label{s:general_LinBitsDual}

\renewcommand{\modulemorphism}{\psi}
\renewcommand{\ringmorphism}{\psi}
\renewcommand{\moduleonebound}{S_{\leq B}}

We now proceed to describe a generalization of $\LinBitsDual(\bits, \pi, R, \cC)$ where the witness $\bff$ has entries in an arbitrary ring $S$ (finitely generated as a $\ZZ_{\Char(S)}$-module),  instead of in $\bits\subseteq \ZZ$.  

The witness entries will be restricted to a bounded subset of $S$, so that each witness can be expressed as a pre-specified linear expression (over $S$) of a string of bits.


We assume the notation from the previous sections, together with:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
 \item Let $S$ be a ring, possibly different than $R$, let $\psi:S \to R$ be a ring homomorphism. %Let $M$ be a $S$-module and  let $\psi: \moduleone \to R$ be a module homomorphism (with underlying ring homomorphism $\ringmorphism$ \albert{terminology in preliminaries}).
\item 
Let $\gamma_1,\ldots, \gamma_\moduledegree\in S$ be a fixed generating set of $S$ (\cref{s:algebra}), for some $\moduledegree\geq 1$ . Fix a vector length $\codedim_0$.  Let $\moduleonebound$ denote the set of elements of $S$ of the form $h=\sum_{i\in [\moduledegree]} h_i\cdot \gamma_i$ for $$h_i \in \range{0}{2^B-1}_S \subseteq \ZZ_{\Char(S)} \subseteq  S$$ (cf.\ \cref{s:prelims_algebra}) \item Let $\bff \in \moduleonebound^{\codedim_0}$, and write %$\bff=(\bff_{*1}, \ldots, \bff_{*\moduledegree})$ 
$\bff=\sum_{j\in [\moduledegree]} \bff_{*j}\cdot \gamma_j$
for some $\bff_{*j}\in \ZZ_{\Char(S)}^{\codedim_0}$, $j\in [\moduledegree]$ (note that  any ring $S$ contains $\ZZ_{\Char(S)}$ as a subring).  Then write $$\bff_{*j}= \sum_{k=1}^{B}\  \bff_{*jk}\cdot 2^{k-1} $$ for some vectors $$\bff_{*jk}\in \{0,1\}_S^{\codedim_0}\subseteq S^{\codedim_0},$$ for all $j,k$.  We define   $$\bitstext(\bff)\in \bits^{\codedim_0\cdot\moduledegree\cdot  B} \subseteq \ZZ^{\codedim_0\cdot\moduledegree\cdot B}$$ as the vector with integer bit entries obtained by concatenating the vectors $\bff_{*jk}$ and lifting their entries to $\bits \subseteq \ZZ$ in a natural way, i.e.\ \begin{equation}\label{e:bits_of_f}\bitstext(\bff) \defeq \left(\mathsf{lift}(\bff_{*jk})\mid  j\in [\moduledegree], k\in [B]\right)\in \bits^{\codedim_0\cdot\moduledegree\cdot B} \subseteq \ZZ^{\codedim_0\cdot\moduledegree\cdot B},\end{equation}
where $\mathsf{lift}$ is the identity function if $S=\ZZ$,  and  $\mathsf{lift} = \pi_{\Char(S)}^{-1}$ if $\Char(S)>0$.

\begin{remark}[Non-uniform bit-decomposition sizes]\label{r: non_uniform_bit_decomposition} In practical settings, it is possible that not all entries of $\bff$ have the same bit size. For example, some chunks of $\bff$ may be comprised of elements from $\{0,1\}_S$, while others may be comprised of generic ring elements. However, our definition of $\bitstext(\bff)$ allocates $\moduledegree\cdot B$ bits for every entry of $\bff$, no matter its size.

In this case one can define $\bitstext(\bff)$  as a vector from $\bits^{\codedim}\subseteq \ZZ^\codedim$ (for a some  $\codedim$) such that 
%
$$
L\cdot \pi_{\Char(S)}(\bitstext(\bff)) = \bff \in S^{\codedim_0}
$$
for some public and fixed matrix $L\in S^{\codedim_0 \times \codedim}$.
%

This generalizes the previous definition of $\mathsf{bits}(\bff)$, in that, for such definition, we have $L\cdot \pi_{\Char(S)}(\bitstext(\bff)) = \bff$ where $$L=\mathsf{Id}_{\codedim_0\times \codedim_0} \otimes \pi_{\Char(S)}(1, 2, \ldots, 2^{B-1}) \otimes (\gamma_1, \ldots, \gamma_d) \in S^{\codedim_0\times (\codedim_0\cdot B\cdot d)},$$
%
where $\mathsf{Id}_{\codedim_0\times \codedim_0} $ is the $\codedim_0\times \codedim_0$ identity matrix. 
\end{remark}
For a general matrix $L$, one needs, for efficiency purposes, to make sure $L$ has enough structure to later support  tensor decomposing certain vectors, as discussed in \cref{r:tensor_dec_u}. In any case, one can always use the matrix $L$ above, which does possess enough structure.
\item 
Assume $\cC$ is a linear code over $\comfield$ of dimension $(\codedim_0\cdot B \cdot \moduledegree)/\comfieldexp$ and blocklength $\codelength$. 
\end{itemize}



\begin{definition} The \emph{general Lin-BitsRing}  relation is defined as \albertimportant{Here we should allow for $\psi = \phi \circ \picanon^{S}{}^{-1}$ for $\phi: \ZZ[X_1, \ldots, X_k]\to R$ a morphism. This is what is actually happening and it allows us to build later full IOPs for constraints over arbitrary f.g.\ rings}
%
\begin{align*}
 &\LinModFieldBin(\moduleonebound,\modulemorphism,R,  \cC)\\ &\qquad\qquad\qquad= \left\{ \left(\begin{aligned} &\inp=(\mu, \vv)\in R  \times R^{ \codedim_0}, \\
 &\oracle = f \in \comfield^{\codelength}, \\ &\wit= \bff\in \moduleonebound^{\codedim_0}\end{aligned}\right) \;\middle|\;  \begin{aligned}  
 &f = \Enc_{\cC}\circ \pack\circ\pi_2(\bitstext(\bff)),\\
 &\langle \modulemorphism(\bff), \vv \rangle = \mu  \qquad  \text{in } R
 \end{aligned} \right\}.
\end{align*}
\end{definition}
%
When there is no risk of confusion, we denote $\LinModFieldBin(\moduleonebound,\modulemorphism,\moduletwo,  \cC)$ simply by $\LinModFieldBin$. The Core Lin-BitsRing relation is precisely $\LinModFieldBin(\moduleonebound,\modulemorphism,\moduletwo,  \cC)$ after taking $S=\ZZ$, $B=1$, and $\modulemorphism = \pi_{\Char(R)}$.

%\renewcommand{\modulemorphism}
\subsection{The \ftwoz\  IOP for the general Lin-BitsRing relation}\label{s: general_bitz}



Next we describe our IOP for $\LinModFieldBin$.  We assume the notation of \cref{s:general_LinBitsDual}. Let $\codedim_1,\codedim_2$ be tensor decomposition parameters (to be used in \cref{c:core_iop}) such that $\codedim_1\cdot \codedim_2= \codedim_0\cdot \moduledegree\cdot B$. We assume that $\cC$ is a linear code over $\comfield$ of dimension $\codedim_0\cdot \moduledegree\cdot B/\comfieldexp$. %, which is a $\codedim_2$-interleaving of a code $\cC_1$ over $\comfield$ of dimension $\codedim_1/2^{\comfieldexp}$.

We will need the following result.


\begin{lemma}\label{l:bitify}
    Let $\bff\in \moduleonebound^{\codedim_0}$, and let $\vv\in \moduletwo^{\codedim_0}$. Then \albertimportant{General comment: I need to discuss how the verifier handles these vectors $\vv$ and $\uu$. Reviewers may complain that verifier could be non-succinct.}
       \begin{align*}
   & \langle \modulemorphism(\bff), \vv \rangle = \langle \ringmorphism(\bitstext(\bff)_S),\uu\rangle \in R \\ & 
    \uu\defeq \psi(L)^{\mathsf{T}}\cdot \vv \in R^{\codedim_0\cdot B\cdot \moduledegree},
    \end{align*}
    %
    where $\bitstext(\bff)_S \defeq \pi_{\Char(S)}(\bitstext(\bff))$ is  the vector $\bitstext(\bff)$ projected to $\{0,1\}_S\subseteq S$, and $L$ is the matrix from \cref{r: non_uniform_bit_decomposition}.  In particular, if $L=\mathsf{Id}_{\codedim_0 \times \codedim_0} \otimes \pi_{\Char(S)}(1,\ldots, 2^{B-1})\otimes (\gamma_1, \ldots, \gamma_d) \in S^{\codedim_0 \times (\codedim_0\cdot B\cdot \moduledegree)}$, then \begin{equation}\label{e:u_def}
    \uu =   \left(\vv \otimes \left(1, \ringmorphism(2), \ldots, \ringmorphism(2^{B-1})\right) \otimes \left(\modulemorphism(\gamma_1), \ldots, \modulemorphism(\gamma_{\moduledegree})\right)\right) \in R^{\codedim_0\cdot \moduledegree \cdot B}
    \end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Rewriting $\langle \modulemorphism(\bff), \vv \rangle$, we obtain  by \cref{r: non_uniform_bit_decomposition,e:matrix_transpose_inner_product}
$$
\langle \psi(\bff), \vv\rangle = \langle \psi(L\cdot \bitstext(\bff)_S), \vv\rangle =  \langle \psi(\bitstext(\bff)_S), \psi(L)^{\mathsf{T}} \cdot \vv\rangle 
$$
%
The specific form of $\uu$ in \eqref{e:u_def} is obtained by direct calculation of $\psi(L)^{\mathsf{T}} \cdot \vv$.
%
\begin{comment}
\begin{align}
    \langle \modulemorphism(\bff),\vv\rangle &= \langle \modulemorphism\left(\sum_{\substack{k\in [B],\\ j\in [\moduledegree]}} \bff_{*jk} \cdot 2^{k-1} \cdot \gamma_j\right), \vv \rangle\label{e:iop_linear_claim_1}\\ &= \langle \sum_{\substack{k\in [B],\\ j\in [\moduledegree]}} \ringmorphism(\bff_{*jk}) \cdot \ringmorphism(2^{k-1}) \cdot \modulemorphism(\gamma_j),\vv \rangle,\label{e:iop_linear_claim_2}\\
    &= \sum_{\substack{k\in [B],\\ j\in [\moduledegree]}} \ringmorphism(2^{k-1}) \cdot \modulemorphism(\gamma_j)\cdot \langle \ringmorphism(\bff_{*jk}), \vv \rangle \notag\\ &=   \sum_{\substack{k\in [B],\\ j\in [\moduledegree]}} \sum_{i\in [\codedim_0]} \ringmorphism(2^{k-1}) \cdot \modulemorphism(\gamma_j)\cdot  \langle \ringmorphism(\bff_{ijk}), \vv_i \rangle= \langle \ringmorphism(\bitstext(\bff)), \uu \rangle \notag,
\end{align}
as needed.
\end{comment}
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[IOP for $\LinModFieldBin$]\label{c:LinModFieldBin}

 \textbf{Parameters.} $$(S, R, \ringmorphism, \codedim_0, B, \codedim, L, \codedim_1,\codedim_2, \comfield,\cC)$$ comprised of: two rings $S, R$; a ring morphism $\ringmorphism:S \to R$;  a witness length $\codedim_0$; a bit-size bound $B$; a bit number $\codedim$; a \emph{bitification} matrix $L\in S^{\codedim_0\times \codedim}$ as in \cref{r: non_uniform_bit_decomposition}; tensor decomposition dimensions $\codedim_1, \codedim_2$ for \cref{c:core_iop} satisfying $\codedim_1\cdot \codedim_2 = \codedim$; a binary field $\comfield$; a linear code $\cC$ over $\comfield$ of dimension $\codedim/\comfieldexp$ and blocklength $\codelength$.\\




\noindent \textbf{Inputs.} The prover and verifier receive $\inp=(\mu, \vv)\in \moduletwo\times R^{\codedim_0}$ and  $\oracle= f \in \comfield^\codelength$. The prover receives  $\wit= \bff\in \moduleonebound^{\codedim_0}$ such that $(\inp,\oracle, \wit) \in \LinModFieldBin(\moduleonebound,\modulemorphism, \moduletwo, \cC)$, i.e., 
%
$$
f = \Enc_{\cC}\circ \pack\circ\pi_2(\bitstext(\bff)),\qquad \langle \modulemorphism(\bff), \vv \rangle = \mu \quad \text{in } R.
$$
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*]
     \item By \cref{l:bitify}, the   claim $(\inp,\oracle, \wit) \in \LinModFieldBin(\moduleonebound,\modulemorphism, R,  \cC)$ is equivalent to the claim \begin{equation}\label{e:iop_final_linear_claim}
     \left(\inp'=(\mu, \uu), \oracle' = f=\Enc_{\cC}\circ \pack\circ\pi_2(\bitstext(\bff)), \wit' = \bitstext(\bff)\right) \in \LinBitsDual(\bits,\pi, R,\cC).
\end{equation}
where $\uu = \psi(L)^{\mathsf{T}}\cdot \vv$. %  \left(\vv \otimes \left(1, \ringmorphism(2), \ldots, \ringmorphism(2^{B-1})\right) \otimes \left(\modulemorphism(\gamma_1), \ldots, \modulemorphism(\gamma_{\moduledegree})\right)\right)$. 
Accordingly, prover and verifier use \cref{c:core_iop} to prove \eqref{e:iop_final_linear_claim}. They use parameters $(R,\comfield,\codedim,\codedim_1,\codedim_2,\cC)$.  See \cref{r:tensor_dec_u} for comments on how to use tensor decompositions of $\uu$ in \cref{c:core_iop}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{construction}


\begin{remark}[Tensor decompositions of $\uu$]\label{r:tensor_dec_u}
Recall that \cref{c:core_iop} considers a (possibly trivial) tensor decomposition of its public input vector $\uu$ (denoted $\vv$ in \cref{c:core_iop}). Moreover, as argued in \cref{s:to_low_entropy_gkr,s:gkr_low_entropy}, this tensor decomposition is crucial for performance of \ftwoz (concretely, we want $\codedim^{0.5}\leq \codedim_1 \leq \codedim^{0.6}$, cf.\ \cref{s:gkr_low_entropy}).   

In many applications, the input vector $\vv$ for $\LinModFieldBin(\moduleonebound,\modulemorphism, \moduletwo,  \cC)$ is an ``eq'' vector, i.e.\ a vector of the form $(\eq(\bb,\rr))_{\bb\in\bits^{\log\codedim_0}}$ for some $\rr\in \moduletwo^{\log \codedim_0}$. In this case  $\vv=\vv^{(1)}\otimes \vv^{(2)}$ with the dimensions of $\vv^{(1)}$, $\vv^{(2)}$ being arbitrary as long as $|\vv^{(1)}|\cdot |\vv^{(2)}|=\codedim_0$. In that case, if \begin{equation}\label{e:canonical_bitification_matrix}L=\mathsf{Id}_{\codedim_0\times \codedim_0} \otimes \pi_{\Char(S)}(1,\ldots, 2^{B-1})\otimes (\gamma_1,\ldots, \gamma_\moduledegree)\end{equation} (cf.\ \cref{r: non_uniform_bit_decomposition}), then $\uu$ has the form \eqref{e:u_def} and we may use a tensor decomposition for $\uu$ in \cref{c:core_iop} of the form, say, $\uu=\uu^{(1)}\otimes \uu^{(2)}$, where
%
$$
\uu^{(1)} \defeq \vv^{(1)},\quad \uu^{(2)}\defeq \vv^{(2)}\otimes \left(1, \ringmorphism(2), \ldots, \ringmorphism(2^{B-1})\right) \otimes \left(\modulemorphism(\gamma_1), \ldots, \modulemorphism(\gamma_{\moduledegree})\right).
$$
%
For more general matrices $L$, one needs to make sure that $L$ has enough structure to allow for a favorable tensor decomposition of $\uu$. If that is not possible, one can always use the matrix \eqref{e:canonical_bitification_matrix}.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}\label{t:thm_LinModFieldBits}
    Under the same hypotheses and notation as in \cref{t:thm_core_IOPP}, \cref{c:LinModFieldBin} is a perfectly complete $(2+t_\red + t_\iopp)$-round IOP for $\LinModFieldBin(\moduleonebound,\modulemorphism, \moduletwo, \cC)$ with round-by-round knowledge soundness with relaxation $(\LinModFieldBin^{\delta},\bot)$ and errors $$\kappa_{\mathsf{OOD}}, \kappa_0, (\ksred_i)_{i\in [t_\red]}, (\ksiopp_i)_{i\in [t_\iopp]},$$ where $\kappa_{\mathsf{OOD}}$ is as in \cref{t:thm_core_IOPP} and $
    \kappa_0 = \theta(R,\codedim). %\frac{\degevalfield}{q_{\min}} + \frac{\log(\codedim_1 +1) + \log(q-1)}{|\cP|\cdot \lfloor\log(q_{\min})\rfloor}.
    $ (see \cref{t:thm_core_IOPP}; here $\theta(R,\codedim)$ is computed with respect to the tensor decomposition $\uu=\uu^{(1)}\otimes \uu^{(2)}$ of \cref{r:tensor_dec_u} used in \cref{c:core_iop}, i.e.\ the quantities $D$, $T$, $N_R$ are determined by the lifts of the entries of $\uu^{(1)}$ and $\uu^{(2)}$).
    
    The error terms $\kappa_{\mathsf{OOD}}, \kappa_0$ are void if Round 0 or Round 1 of \cref{c:core_iop} are not executed, respectively. In that case \cref{c:LinModFieldBin} has one or two rounds less.
    %If $\degevalfield=0$, i.e.\ $\evalfield$ is a prime field, then \cref{c:core_iop} is a $t_\red+t_\iopp$-round IOP with round-by-round knowledge soundness with relaxation $(\LinBitsDual^\delta, \bot)$, with errors $\kappa_0, (\ksred_i)_{i\in [t_\red]}, (\ksiopp_i)_{i\in [t_\iopp]}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof} Deferred to \cref{a:LinModFieldBin}. In short, the result is a consequence of \cref{l:bitify}, which establishes an equivalence between  triples for $\LinModFieldBin(\moduleonebound,\modulemorphism, \moduletwo, \cC)$ and $\LinBitsDual(\bits,\pi, \moduletwo,\cC)$. \cref{l:bitify} further provides a method for efficiently computing one triple from the other. This allows to build a round-by-round knowledge soundness state and an extractor for \cref{c:LinModFieldBin} from those of \cref{c:core_iop}.
\end{proof}



\subsection{Instantiation}\label{s:instantiation}
We instantiate \cref{c:core_iop} by letting $\Pi_{\red}$ be an optimized GKR protocol for multiple grand product claims of certain form. The IOP $\Pi_\iopp$ is taken to be WHIR \cite{whir} with the ring switching step from \cite{binius} (see \cref{a:ring_switch_1}).

\bigskip

We next provide example  instantiations of \cref{c:core_iop,c:LinModFieldBin} so as to achieve  $\lambda=128$ bits of security, while keeping the protocols as efficient as possible, with the main challenge being to use $\comfield$ of size $2^{128}$. We remark that in \cref{?} we provide experimental results both for $128$ and $100$ bits of security.   Achieving $\lambda=100$ bits of security is relatively straightforward and we do not discuss it. We use a careful selection of parameters in combination with grinding. In \cref{r:beyond_gridning} we discuss other instantiations and techniques that do not require grinding.

The contents of this section should be taken as examples of how to instantiate \cref{c:core_iop}, not as limiting strict rules and settings. Other configurations may be perfectly plausible and practical. 

\bigskip


In terms of security, we need to ensure the following:
\begin{enumerate}
       \item The round-by-round knowledge soundness errors of \cref{c:core_iop} $\kappa_{\mathsf{OOD}}, \kappa_0, (\ksred_i)_{i}, (\ksiopp_i)_{i}$ are all at most $2^{-\lambda}$.
    \item The round-by-round knowledge soundness errors of \cref{c:core_iop} $\kappa_0, (\ksred_i)_{i}, (\ksiopp_i)_{i}$ are all at most $2^{-\lambda}$. 
\end{enumerate}
%


In terms of efficiency, the most important step of \cref{c:core_iop} to be aware of is Phase 3, where one uses an IOR $\Pi_{\red}$ to reduce the claim 
\begin{equation}\label{e: grand_products_again}
\prod_{i \in [\codedim_1]} \left(\left(g^{\bm{\gamma}_i}-1\right)\cdot \pi_2(\bff_{ij}) +1\right)= g^{\mu_j}  \quad  \in \comfield, \qquad \text{for all } j\in [\codedim_2],
\end{equation}
into a linear claim on $\pi(\bff)$. As mentioned, in our instantiation, $\Pi_{\red}$ is the GKR protocol for multiple grand product claims,
%
equipped with the optimizations discuss in \cref{s:gkr_low_entropy}. This is the most expensive prover part of \cref{c:core_iop,c:LinModFieldBin}, constituting between $50\%$ to $66\%$ of prover costs, cf.\ \cref{?}. Because of this, it is important for efficiency that operating in $\comfield$ is as efficient as possible. 

With this in mind, we target using $\comfield$ with $\comfieldexp\approx 128$. A natural candidate is the $2^{128}$ sized binary field in GHASH representation  $$\comfield= \FF_2[X]/(X^{128} + X^7 + X^2 + X + 1).$$ 
%
%
Before anything else, we discuss the constraint  $\max\cP < \fieldboundsizeexponent$. 
%
First of all, we set $\codedim_1, \codedim_2$ with $\codedim_1\cdot \codedim_2 = \codedim$ and 
%
\begin{equation}\label{e:codedim_1_proportion}
\codedim_1 \in [\codedim^{0.5}, \codedim^{0.6}].
\end{equation}
%
In general, choosing $\codedim_1 \approx \codedim^{0.6}$ can lead to better prover time and proof size for the GKR grand product step, as observed in our experiments. However choosing $\codedim_1\approx \codedim^{0.5}$ allows to use slightly smaller soundness errors, which reduces grinding times if targeting $128$ bits of security.  


%
%To ground our analysis, we two witness bounds $\codedim\leq2^{35}$ (so the witness consists of $\leq 2^{35}$ bits), say, and $\codedim_1 \leq 2^{21}$ following our rule of thumb.   


We now discuss how to make the errors $\kappa_{\mathsf{OOD}}, \kappa_0, (\ksred_i)_{i}, (\ksiopp_i)_{i}$ be all at most $2^{-\lambda}$.



\paragraph{The errors $\kappa_{i,\iopp}$.} Regarding Phase 4, we instantiate our IOP for $\LIN(\comfield, \pack, \cC)$ as the WHIR protocol, with the ring switching from \cite{binius}  (see \cref{a:ring_switch_1}). The ring switching step adds an error term of the form $1/\comfield$. If targetting 128 bits of security, we use grinding  (\cref{s:IORs})  of $1$ bit in this round. WHIR is configured at any desired $\lambda\leq 128$ bits of security using $|\comfield|=2^{128}$ following \cite{stir-repo,whir-repo}. We do not discuss this further since WHIR is a well-known IOP, and it is well-kown that it can achieve $128$ bits of security in $128$ bit fields when codewords have dimension approximately $2^{30}$ (assuming an interleaved RS code).


\paragraph{The error $\kappa_{\mathsf{OOD}}$.} As mentioned, we instantiate $\Pi_\iopp$  as WHIR with the ring-switching techniques of \cite{binius} and \cref{a?}. We use a proximity parameter up to the Johnson bound, hence Round 0 of \cref{c:core_iop} is executed and contributes the error $\kappa_{\mathsf{OOD}}\leq \binom{L_\delta}{2}\cdot (\codedim/\comfieldexp-1)/|\comfield|$ (\cref{t:thm_core_IOPP}). For a Reed--Solomon code of rate $\rho$ and $\delta=1-\sqrt{\rho}-\eta$, we have the list size bound \albert{review} $L_\delta \leq 1/(2\eta\sqrt{\rho})$. We select $\eta=??$, which leads to an error $\kappa_{\mathsf{OOD}}\leq **$, for which, if needed, we can attain 128 bits of security by grinding $??$ bits \albertimportant{fill in the ?? numbers}. 

\paragraph{The errors $\kappa_{i,\red}$.} As mentioned, our $\Pi_{\red}$ IOR is the GKR protocol for multiple grand product claims from \cref{e: grand_products_again}, see \cref{s:gkr_low_entropy}. Each round of the GKR protocol is a round of either a  sumcheck for a polynomial of degree $3$, or a unification of two evaluation claims of the same multilinear polynomial.  Accordingly we have $\ksred_i\leq 3/|\comfield|$ for all $i$. This immediately guarantees $\lambda=100$ bits of security. To achieve $\lambda=128$ bits of security, one can do two bits of grinding (\cref{s:IORs}) per round. 

\paragraph{The error $\kappa_0$.}
 The error $\kappa_0$ is perhaps the most delicate, as it demands at the same time that  $|\cP|$ is large, while still satisfying $\max\cP < \fieldboundsizeexponent$.
  
 Recall that in many scenarios $\kappa_0$ does not exist in the IOP  (i.e.\ sometimes our IOP skips Round 1). Concretely, if our initial inner product claim is over $R=\FF_q$ a prime field of size $< \fieldboundsizeexponent$, then  $\kappa_0$  does not appear as an error term, and we do not need to do anything to adjust for it. The term $\kappa_0$ does appear in all other scenarios ($R=\ZZ$ with large or negative entries in $\vv^{(1)}$  \albert{negative?} (cf.\ \cref{r:skip_round_1_integers}), or $R$ a prime field of larger size, or $R$ a proper field extension). We divide our approaches by whether this first step is skipped or not. 


%If $\kappa_0^*$ is indeed an error term present in our scheme, then we have to consider, when targeting $128$ of security, the following restriction: $$\max\cP \leq 2^{128 - 0.6\cdot \log \codedim}.$$ 


\bigskip

\noindent For the purposes of this discussion, we assume \ftwoz \ is used within a bigger proof system, as described in \cref{s:to_simple_bitz} or \cref{s:iop_upesat}, so that \ftwoz \ is used to prove an inner product claim for a committed witness, as the last phase of a bigger proving system where 1) first the initial constraint is projected to a finite field $\FF$, or, alternatively, this step is skipped if the constraint is  already over a finite field; and 2) a  PIOP is run over $\FF$ for the projected constraint, outputting an inner product claim on the projected witness. Now, this inner product claim is the input of \cref{c:core_iop}, and we aim to configure \cref{c:core_iop} at 128 bits of security for this setting. 


\paragraph{Strategy 1: the PIOP achieves $128$ bits of security through grinding and we avoid the error term $\kappa_0$.} If allowing, say, $18-19$ bits of grinding during each round of the PIOP (which adds $1.8-3.5$ms per round, cf.\ \cref{tab:grinding-cost}), then, say, a Spartan-like PIOP will achieve $128$ bits of security if run on a field $\FF$ of size $\approx 2^{112}$ to  $2^{113}$  (since each one of its rounds is either a sumcheck round for a degree $\leq 3$ polynomial, or a step that batches some claims with error $1/|\FF|$). Hence, if the initial constraint is over $\ZZ$ or $\ZZ[X]$, then we may project it to a $112$-$113$ bit random prime field $\FF$ and run a PIOP over $\FF$. We study this case next. Our analysis also applies if the original constraint was already expressed over a prime field of such size.  

Note that the projection of the constraint to $\FF$ also requires to be set up to 128 bits of security. We discuss this in \cref{r:first_projection_security}.




%in this case we may assume $R$  is a finite field of size $|R| \approx 2^{112}- 2^{113}$.  In this latter case, we do need to make sure that this projection step achieves 128 bits of security, while still being able to project to a $112$-$113$ bit field. We discuss this below in this section \cref{?} \albertimportant{do. Also it's a bit weird we discuss the error term of this step, which is a step described later in \cref{s:iop_upesat}}.


Let $q=|\FF|$. Then if
%
$$
q < \fieldboundsizeexponent,
$$
then $\kappa_0$ is not present as an error term, and there is nothing to adjust. Since $|\comfield|=2^{128}$  and $q\approx 2^{113}, 2^{112}$, this occurs as long as $\codedim_1 \lessapprox 2^{15},2^{16}$, and using \eqref{e:codedim_1_proportion}, as long as 
%
$$
\codedim\lessapprox 2^{25}, 2^{26} \qquad \text{if } \codedim_1\approx\codedim^{0.6}
$$
 and
$$
\codedim\lessapprox 2^{30}, 2^{32} \qquad \text{if } \codedim_1\approx\codedim^{0.5}
$$
%
with these upper bounds depending on how much grinding one is doing at the PIOP level. 

\begin{remark}[Projecting polynomial constraints to a $112$-$113$ bit prime field with $128$ bits of security]\label{r:first_projection_security}
    \albertimportant{Need to clean up the UPESAT section first}
\end{remark}

\bigskip 
%Belo only analyse the scenario where $R$ is a finite field, and thus do not consider the case $R=\ZZ$, which is unlikely to occur in practice, unless \ftwoz\ is used in isolation, and not as a step subsequent to a PIOP. 





\paragraph{Strategy 2: No or essentially no grinding during the PIOP, and $\kappa_0$ is present and  grinding is used during the first projection step of \cref{c:core_iop}.} We now assume $\FF$ is either a prime field with  $|\FF|\geq \fieldboundsizeexponent$,  or a proper extension field. In this case  \cref{c:core_iop} goes through an initial projection step with soundness error  $\kappa_0$.

Recall (\cref{t:thm_core_IOPP}) that $\kappa_0 = \theta(\FF, \codedim)$. We use the one-generator presentation of $\FF$ from \cref{e:presentations}, with the natural lifts, so that $k=1$ and, denoting by $d$ the degree of $\FF$ over its prime field, $D=d-1$, $T\leq d$, and $N_\FF\leq q-1$. We instantiate the morphism sets $\cM_q$ as in \cref{l:examples_morphisms}, i.e.\ $\pirand$ reduces coefficients modulo a sampled prime and evaluates $X_1$ at a random $\alpha\in \FF_q$. In this case, by \cref{l:examples_morphisms},
    $$
    \kappa_0 \leq \frac{2\cdot (d-1)}{q_{\min}} + \frac{\log\left(8\cdot d\cdot \codedim\cdot (q-1)^2\right)}{|\cP|\cdot \lfloor\log(q_{\min})\rfloor},
      $$
    where $q$ is  the characteristic of $\FF$.

%
    %The adjustment for $\kappa_0^*$ (if needed at all) is highly dependent on $\degevalfield$. Concretely, if $\degevalfield=0$, then the term $\degevalfield/q_{\min}$ is zero. This allows to take $\cP$ as all primes smaller than $2^{114+\eps}$. There are approximately $2^{114}/114$ of these.
%
    We select $\cP$ to be the set of all primes in a range $[2^a, 2^b]$. There are approximately  $$|\cP|\approx (2^b/b - 2^a/a)/\log_e(2)$$ such primes.  Using $b= 128 - \tau \cdot \log(\codedim)$,  $\codedim_1\leq 2^{\tau\cdot \log(\codedim)}$,  for $\tau \in \range{0.5}{0.6}$ and plugging these values into $\kappa_0$ we obtain
     %
     $$
     \begin{aligned}
    \kappa_0\lessapprox f(a)&\defeq \frac{2\cdot (d-1)}{2^a} + \frac{\log\left(8\cdot d\cdot \codedim\cdot (q-1)^2\right)\cdot \log_e(2)}{\frac{a}{b}\cdot 2^{b} - 2^a},\\
    b&\defeq 128 - r_1, \qquad r_1\defeq \tau\cdot \log(\codedim).
    \end{aligned}
    $$
    %
    We calculate several minima of $f(a)$ when $\FF$ is a  field of size $\approx 2^{128}$, for varying values of $\codedim$ and $\tau \in \{0.5, 0.6\}$. We include the corresponding grinding times in order to achieve $128$ bits of security for this initial step of \cref{c:core_iop}.  

    %We also include minima of $f(a)$ for other configurations where $R$ is a finite field of size between $2^{128}$ to $2^{256}$, not necessarily a prime field.
